- 2026-10-15 ([docs/design/galaxy/spherical-sector-coordinates.md:3](../design/galaxy/spherical-sector-coordinates.md)): Boss decided to move the galaxy's sector grid from the cylindrical ring/layer/slot grid to spherical shells (Boss, 2026-10-15 06:39Z).
- 2026-10-15 ([docs/design/galaxy/spherical-sector-coordinates.md:24](../design/galaxy/spherical-sector-coordinates.md)): - **Wedges in a band (Boss, 2026-10-15 07:17Z: one rule everywhere, no special regions):** every band of every shell, in the galaxy or in any other shape of sphere, has `W(b) = 6 * m` wedges, where `m` is a number whose prime factors are only 2, 3, 5 and 7 and which is nearest (in ratio) to `2 pi R sin(phi_b) / (6 e)`, `phi_b` being the band's center colatitude; `m` is at least 1.
- 2026-10-15 ([docs/design/galaxy/spherical-sector-coordinates.md:25](../design/galaxy/spherical-sector-coordinates.md)): - **No undefined space (Boss, 2026-10-15 06:59Z):** the core ball, every shell, every cap and every band and wedge together tile all of space: each point has exactly one address, with no gap and no overlap, whether or not that sector has been generated.
- 2026-10-15 ([docs/design/galaxy/spherical-sector-coordinates.md:41](../design/galaxy/spherical-sector-coordinates.md)): Known costs, from the study: face neighbours go from 6 to 7 up to roughly 10 to 15; flat height slabs and the aligned vertical columns are gone, so a region is an arc per (shell, band), not per ring (27 times the range clauses for a 27-wide block); a cap near the dense core holds about 2,800 systems against 1,114 for today's densest cell; the 40-bit birth-sector field of the 80-bit object ID (the first layout; 96 bit
- 2026-10-15 ([docs/design/galaxy/spherical-shell-sectors.md:5](../design/galaxy/spherical-shell-sectors.md)): **Update, 2026-10-15 06:39Z:** Boss decided to go ahead with spherical shells, with pole caps and wedges starting at a 4 pc gap.

- 2026-10-18 ([docs/design/operations/cache-warm-up.md:78](../design/operations/cache-warm-up.md)): **A tile is built again only when what it holds changed (PERF.133, Boss 2026-10-18 6:05 PM EDT).** The stored tiles are keyed by `query.galaxy_tile_stamp`, a hash of the galaxy shape, the bright-star seed, the naming key, `query.TILE_FORMAT`, the generator epoch and the change counters; the release number is not in it (it stays in `galaxy_content_stamp`, which the pages and counts follow).

- 2026-10-24 ([docs/plan/lanes.md:144](../plan/lanes.md)): - Surface pressure supply law first (Boss, 3:50 PM EDT 2026-10-24: 'Hold 4c until the supply law is done', no stand-in pressure): order 4b, then GEN.726 (supply law, Lane I), then 4c (GEN.411 + GEN.422 as one merge), then GEN.557.
- 2026-10-24 ([docs/plan/lanes.md:155](../plan/lanes.md)): - A lane that waits on a blocker starts the next unblocked item of its tree; a lane retires the day its tree is done; items over about an hour of gate are sliced; changed-tests gate with --resume for every merge (Boss, 8:07 PM and 8:10 PM EDT 2026-10-24: no whole-suite gate for ordinary merges, no second gate on the merge candidate; reports/lane-testing-audit-2026-10-24.md; the full run is the end-of-version bug hunt

- 2026-10-30 ([docs/design/habitability/condition-based-planet-classes.md:47](../design/habitability/condition-based-planet-classes.md)): Each instead became a condition that keeps its old meaning: K the cold thin-air world (below freezing, usually under ice), L the thin oxygen-bearing marginal world, S the airless super-Earth (size), V the high-gravity world (2 g or more since GEN.1136; at 1.25 g it took most temperate super-Earths and left M almost empty). **Q is retired** (Boss, 6:07 PM EDT 2026-10-30, GEN.1135): a class that was only an orbit.
